## Sully

Sully was a thug from the criminal underbelly of Waterdeep circa 1490s DR.

Personality
Sully was not afraid in getting his hands dirty and could go against the City Watch for a right price or in exchange for a right favor.

Activities
Sully had many connections within Waterdeep's crime organizations as well as more dangerous affiliations with the tomb-robbers in Neverwinter.

History
Sometime in the 1490s DR, before the case of Laeral Silverhand's stolen baby griffon, Sully was acquainted with the halfling thief that later was recruited against his will to aid the Open Lord's stolen pet's retrieval. If the halfling thief were to fail his mission and flee Waterdeep, Sully was ready to lend him a hand in exchange for the little rogue's help with robbing a certain dwarven tomb just outside of Neverwinter.

When the iron crisis struck the Sword Coast, in the, Surrey left Beregost in search of better prospects. He traveled north to the Friendly Arm inn, en route to the great metropolis of Waterdeep.

## Suranatra

Suranatra was the first capital city of the Kuong Kingdom in Malatra, later deserted and fallen into ruins. It lay among the southern foothills of the Akashnu Mountains.Like many places in the Kuong Kingdom, Suranatra and the Akashnu Mountains have not been marked on any map, so their location is unknown.

History
The ancient city of Suranatra was where the Kuong Kingdom first arose, founded by Vishnan I with it as his capital. From the outset, it was an aggressive and theocratic empire. Funded by the gems and precious metals of the Akashnu Mountains, the rich and puissant priest-kings of Suranatra expanded their dominion to the Kunong River and to the coast.

However, as the Kuong Kingdom expanded, it was forced to relocate its capital to Parmahana for administrative purposes. The people demolished part of the city, ironically to prevent its temples and holy sites from being desecrated after they left.

Description
By the mid–14 century DR, the jungle had reclaimed the ruins of Suranatra, though not as much as its successor, Parmahana. Wanderers and criminals used these ruins as a place to hide.

## Sunmelon

Sunmelons, also known as watermelons, were sweet and succulent fruits that grew on vines and were cultivated in many lands throughout the Realms.

Description
Sunmelons found in the north were known for their succulent and refreshingly sweet flesh encased in a green striped skin. The fruit grew on vines and ripened on the ground. They often were simply eaten raw, and sometimes pickled or jellied.

The type of sunmelon cultivated in the Vilhon Reach, however, was different. The similarly sweet fruit was golden in color and encased in golden skin. The melon was fleshier than its northern cousin and the mouthfeel of Vilhon sunmelons was gentle, as if the fruit melted on the tongue.

Availability

Deepingdale, in the Dalelands, was a perfect place for growing sunmelons and pumpkins. With the region's decline, the watermelons that grew there became smaller, about the size of a sweet potato.

Sunmelons were cultivated in Delimbiyr Vale and Chionthar Valley, and were one of the regions' trade goods.

In the exotic-goods markets of Calimshan, sunmelons were a common sight.

In the Blade Kingdoms, wild watermelon could be foraged by hungry travelers and adventures on the road.

In Sembia, sunmelon rind was a food found in taverns and served along with many other small bite-sized treats.

The Sunwalker's Gift was an enchanted ring that could be used as a source of daylight.

The Sunwalker's Gift was a simple silvery metal band with a single blood-red gem stone embedded into it.

In 1492 DR, the Sunwalker's Gift was in possession of a orog member of the Society of Brilliance, Blurg, who was operating out of a peaceful myconid colony in the Underdark, working to improve the living conditions of the subterranean realm.

## Sunfirth

Sunfirth was a barmaid at the Bold Bard tavern in the town of Ghars in Cormyr in the mid–14 century DR.

Activities
Working for Shortshanks, Sunfirth delivered drinks and food and cleaned up the same after the brawls that broke out between patrons. She also recorded the takings and the cost of damages in the account book.

History
Sunfirth was working at the Bold Bard on Eleint 16 in the, and was left to clean up after a brawl between Rolf and Dovo, and probably when Dovo spat on the floor later. The next night it was Rolf and Jasper, and then she tried to chase and whack the mice Jasper secretly released as a ruse while he stole the account book. The night after, while obsessing on a murder case in his confused dreams, Jasper briefly theorized that an axe-wielding Sunfirth beheaded Dovo for the mess and Grodoveth for not tipping. Which, while unlikely, didn't seem unreasonable given what she put up with.

Description
Sunfirth stood around  tall and weighed about, suggesting she was not in fact an axe-murderer.

## Sundamar's tower

Sundamar's Tower was a mage tower found in Myth Drannor, and the personal residence of the eccentric wizard Sundamar.

Location
The tower was located high above Eldansyr's Ride, in the city's Sheshyrinnam district.

Structure
The four-story stone tower was not built atop the ground, or even as an addition to one of the city's massive shadowtops, but rather appeared to grow out of the prolific and appropriately-named Towersclutch Tree.

The foundation of the tower rest some  above ground level, offering little protection against winds that regularly shook the branches of the tree, and the tower with them.

Rumors & Legends
Some people believed that the tower's unique construction was not entirely intentional, and may have been a result of one of Sundamar's failed experiments, perhaps one involving spelljamming-related magics.

## Sunder Halyndliam

Sunder Halyndliam, shortened over time to Liam, was a powerful wizard and warrior who lived in his tower one day's travel from Daggerford on the Sword Coast. The town of Liam's Hold was named in his memory.

History
Sunder Halyndliam was still alive circa 972 DR. During that time, a duo of renowned thieves from Calimshan - Jamzo and Jemzo Bakadi, attempted to pull off the heist of a lifetime. Evidently, the brothers attempted to rob Sunder Halyndliam. The thieves were never seance after that. The warrior wizard, captured unlucky Bakadi brothers and used their skulls to craft a powerful enchanted item known as the Bone Brothers Bakadi. The magic skulls were stashed in Sunder's crypt.

At some point before his death, Sunder captured a drow worshiper of Vhaeraun, Sieronius Dethick, who was on a mission to the surface world. The warrior-wizard forced the drow to consumed vile ichor that slowly drained Sieronius' life and transformed him into a vampire. Sunder Halyndliam cursed the drow vampire to be forever bound to the tomb underneath Liam's Hold with only Sieronius' wife being able to release him. The drow remained trapped there for many centuries.

Liam was buried in a spell-guarded crypt deep under the tower, and was said to be buried with all of his magical possessions. These included his spellbooks, magic rings, magical staff, and enchanted blade. By the mid-1360s DR, his crypt hadn't been plundered by anyone, owing to the other-planar creatures that guarded his tomb and the denial of entry by locals. The locals did this because they believed that Liam's magic held the tower together and if Liam's magic items were removed the tower would crumble, destroying the town in the falling stone. This was why all of Liam's magical items were buried with him. His silent armored wraith was said to still defend his tower against intruders, and six thieves had been found dead at the base of the tower.

## Sumbrar

Sumbrar was an island located near Evermeet.

Description
Sumbrar was the headquarters for the military and navy of Evermeet. The island was home to mighty magical fortresses, interconnected with each other.

Geography
Sumbrar was a rocky island  to the east of Evermeet. A complicated network of tunnels permeated the island. Large sea caves were found on the coasts of the island.

Defenses
Sumbrar was a fortress and home base to the fleet of Evermeet. Within the tunnels of the islands were barracks that housed up to a thousand warriors. Also based on the island were the aerial forces of Evermeet. The elven aerial troops were housed there along with their giant eagle and pegasus mounts. The navy harbored their ships within massive sea caves.

The island was also home a small flotilla of six spelljammers. These spelljammers, called Ruathimaer, were used to defend Evermeet from attacks from Realmspace.

History
The island was devastated during the Spellplague, when Evermeet was dragged into the Feywild.

Inhabitants
Found deep under the island were twelve ancient dragons: five bronze dragons, four silver dragons and three gold dragons. They, and the warriors who flew them, were in a dreamless sleep, defended from external threats and hidden from observation with powerful magic. Since they could only be awakened once, they were only summoned if the need was dire.

## Sunderland

The Sunderland was a region in southeastern Vaasa where the majority of that realm's Vaasan human and dwarven populations lived.

Description
The Sunderland was the most desirable region for human settlers in Vaasa owing to its combination of soil that could actually support crops and access to mining opportunities in the Galena Mountains. It was a grassland that was covered in snow during the winter months, and became muddy and boggy during the summer.

Geography
The Sunderland was nestled between the mountains where the Galenas and Western Galenas met. It extended as far north as Bloodstone Pass, and included the towns of Darmshall and Maur-Eturo. Hillsafar Hall lay in the mountains on its eastern side.

The mountains along the southern edge of the region were pocked with caves and caverns filled with monsters. The locals called these the Black Holes of Sunderland.

History
Throughout its history prior to the 15 century DR, the land was considered to be unclaimed until someone arrived and began using it. As long as a new settler didn't upset the folks already living nearby, and promised to help out when times got hard or orcs and goblins attacked, they could claim any plot they wanted.

During the reign of Zhengyi the Witch-King from the, until the, he and his armies laid claim to all of Vaasa. Despite this, the Sunderland was never truly conquered as the nigh-impregnable fortress town of Darmshall never fell to the invaders. Regardless, the area was ultimately made a puppet of the Witch-King akin to one of the southern baronies of Damara. During this time, this administrative area was called Sunderland and Darmshall was made the capital. Following the Witch-King's defeat and the collapse of Castle Perilous in 1359 DR, a phenomenon called the Pit began appearing in the Sunderland and claiming victims. Whether this was some new monster or a roving magical trap left behind by the Witch-King was unclear, and bounties were soon offered for anyone who could catch the Pit.

During the early years of his rule, King Gareth Dragonsbane of Damara and his advisors believed it was vitally important to win the hearts and minds of the folk of the Sunderland in order to pacify and annex Vaasa in the wake of the Witch-King's defeat. The Sunderland was eventually absorbed into Gareth Dragonsbane's Kingdom of Bloodstone, and was subsequently conquered by the Warlock Knights of Vaasa in the 1460s DR.

Under the Warlock Knights, the common folk of the Sunderland were reduced to serfs. The lords among the Knights, called Fellthanes, claimed the land and forced the people to work in the fields and hills that they once owned.

A loose coalition of blue dragons was said to lay claim across the entirety of southern Vaasa, which included the Sunderland.

Notable Locations

 The Black Holes of Sunderland
Darmshall
Maur-Eturo
Hillsafar Hall

Inhabitants
The Sunderland was home to small mining communities along the mountains and farming communities further into the grassland, which transitioned into ranching in the northwest near Darmshall and Maur-Eturo. As with anywhere in Vaasa, conditions were harsh and the threat of monster raids was omnipresent, so the people of the Sunderland tended to be hardy and battle-tested. They were also tight-knit and supportive of each other, recognizing that they had to stick together to survive.

Appendix
References

## Surykyk Range

The Surykyk Range was a frozen chain of mountains in southern Alpuk in the Great Glacier.

Description
The slopes of the Surykyk Range were icy and barren, and the wind and snow quickly covered any tracks. Some of the peaks concealed networks of tunnels and caverns beneath them, which connected to the surface via deep fissures and crevasses. These could be up to hundreds of feet (dozens of meters) deep and up to 60 feet (18 meters) wide, and could became treacherously concealed by the snow.

Geography
The mountains formed a southward-angled "V" shape with Mount Akka at the southernmost point. They sat due north of Vaasa beyond the Lugsaas Chain.

As of the late 15 century DR, the Great Glacier had continued its centuries-long retreat northward, and the Surykyks sat on its southern boundary with the newly founded giant kingdom of Kultaakarr along its southern slopes.

Flora & Fauna
The Surykyk Range held popular nesting grounds for kupuk, especially in the south. The mountains' tunnels, caverns, and valleys provided excellent shelter to build their nests and raise their pups, as well as multiple routes to flee or hide from predators, such as the tirichiks that also haunted the caverns and crevasses.

## Summon shadow spirit

Summon shadow spirit was a divine conjuration spell used by priests devoted to Eshowdow.

This particular spell shaped the way of the Eshowe for generations, entangling them in malevolent effects. More than half of the tribe participated in a tribal ceremony involving this spell.

Effects
This spell permanently bound the recipient's shadow into their body and spirit, preventing the recipient from ever casting a natural shadow regardless of the lighting conditions. The Eshowe believed this ceremony bound their shadow to their body, but in fact it bound a shadow from the Negative Energy plane to them instead. The shadow transferred its natural defenses to the recipient, making them immune to spells involving charm, sleep, and hold as well as requiring enchanted weapons to strike them. However, summon shadow spirit also transferred several insidious side effects that twisted their hearts toward evil and had a cumulative chance of actually turning the recipient into a shadow.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component the priest's holy symbol and an area of shadow to cast.

Appendix
References

## Sunstroke

Sunstroke was a divine alteration spell used only by specialty priests devoted to Horus-Re called Sunhawks.

Effects
This spell caused a target to experience heat stroke for the spell's duration, increasing their body temperature by one degree every minute. This spell had no effect on undead or creatures immune to heat or fire. Cold-blooded animals would become more sluggish with each passing minute.

The effects of this spell would cease if dispel magic, remove curse, cure disease, or heal was cast.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component the Sunhawk's holy symbol and a piece of flammable material to cast.

Surkyl was a type of elven meat dish found in the Realms.

Description
This dish consisted of a porcupine that had its belly slashed open, herbs and leeks inserted into it, and then rolled in clay. Finally, the porcupine would be baked in fire so that its hide and quills would be removed alongside its now hardened shell of mud.

## Summon ancestor

Summon ancestor was a divine necromancy spell used by specialty priests devoted to Uthgar, enabling them to call forth a deceased ancestor.

Shamans of Uthgar used this spell on the burial mounds of their tribe which was the only place the spell would function.

Effects
This spell called forth a deceased ancestor from a tribe's burial mound. The ancestor assumed the form of a transparent combination a totem beast and their human form. Normal spirits could also be called forth but could not travel more than  from the burial mound. Once the spirit was summoned the shaman attempted to gain control of it, otherwise the spirt would behave like a vengeful ghost. The ghost was still not able to move more than  from the burial mound.

Controlled spirits were able to attack enemies, as ghosts, guard the ancestor mound for a full year, reveal ancient secrets, initiate a new shaman, and raise the dead.

Components
In addition to verbal components and somatic components, this spell required as material components the priest's holy symbol, a small bonfire, and a relic of the ancestor to cast.

References

## Summon monster V

Summon monster V, also called monster summoning V, was an arcane conjuration spell and one of the series of summon monster spells available to spellcasters .

Effects
This spell summoned a monster to aid the caster. The monster could be a hound archon, an elemental around man-size, an achaierai, bearded devil, shadow mastiff, or a celestial animal: the selection for those included brown bears, stag beetles or griffons. It could also summon a fiendish animal: that animal could be a deinonychus, a dire ape, a dire boar, a monstrous scorpion the size of a horse, a dire wolverine, a giant crocodile or a tiger. In aquatic environments, it could instead summon a celestial sea cat or a fiendish shark the size of an elephant. When cast to summon a creature of a given alignment, the spell's descriptor matched it.

In the version most often used prior to the, up to three creatures were summoned: examples of monsters that could be summoned included ankhegs, cockatrices, displacer beasts, dopplegangers, giant hornets, hydras, leucrottas, subterranean lizards, wereboars, margoyles, minotaurs, ochre jellies, rust monsters, slithering trackers, giant snake, giant spiders, verbeegs, winter volves, and ju-ju zombies. In salt water, it would instead summon giant crocodiles, sea lampreys, scrags, sea lions, selkies, sharks, giant marine spiders, water weirds, and slightly more rarely, silver urchins. In fresh water it would only summon giant crocodiles, giant electric eels, scrags, or water weirds.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components a small bag and tiny candle to cast. The candle did not need to be lit for the spell to function.

History
The spell was originally called Lucke's 5 Monster Summons, and was developed by Lucke in .
This spell was used by Dyrr, the Lichdrow of Menzoberranzan.

## Summon monster VIII

Summon monster VIII was a powerful arcane conjuration spell and one of the series of summon monster spells available to spellcasters. Its use was first recorded around the .

Effects
This spell summoned a powerful extraplanar monster to aid the caster. Besides greater elementals, it could summon lillends, hellcats, or vrocks. It also summoned celestial versions of dire bears or triceratops; fiendish versions of the largest monstrous centipedes, spiders as big as houses, dire tigers or tyrannosaurus could be summoned.

In aquatic environs only, the spell could summon a celestial cachalot whale or fiendish giant squid.

More rarely, some black-hearted casters learned to summon solamiths using this spell.

The version most popular prior to  summoned instead a number of monsters native to the Prime instead. The number varied between one and three in all cases, and there was a delay of up to three minutes: the summons could be answered by giant rhinoceros beetles, fire giants, frost giants, stone golems, gorgimeras, hydras, brown puddings, remorhazs, rust monsters, will-o'-wisps, and purple worms. In freshwater, the summons could be answered by giant sea slugs, dragon turtles, kuo-toa monitors, and mottled worms. In saltwater, it could bring forth eyes of the deep, dragon turtles, krakens, giant sharks, the largest of vodyanoi, killer whales, small herbivore whales, and mottled worms. However, the summoned kraken or a vodyanoi were too willful for the spell's power to control them completely, and were they commanded to act against their nature, there were a slim chance they might spurn the spell's yoke on their intentions and act free-willed for as long as the summon lasted.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as its focus a small bag and tiny candle to cast. The candle did not need to be lit for the spell to function.

History
This spell was used by Dyrr, the Lichdrow of Menzoberranzan.

## Summon spectator

Summon spectator was a divine conjuration spell used by priests devoted to Helm, enabling them to create the ultimate guardian.

Effects
This spell summoned the spectator–a large orb with a central unblinking eye and four eyestalks to monitor each direction. The spectator was corrupted by evil forces during ages past, leading to the formation of the beholder race.

Once summoned, a spectator would serve a single task involving guarding for 101 years. The spectator would only allow its summoner or another priest of Helm to enter the area it was guarding. Its impressive telepathic abilities allowed the spectator to see through false claims quickly.

The spectator would remain at its post unless slain or blinded in all five eyes. If blinded, the spectator would return to Mechanus to regenerate for a full day before returning to its post. However, upon its return if the spectator realized its post had been breached, it would then return permanently to Mechanus.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components the priest's holy symbol and a steel gauntlet blessed by Helm to cast. The gauntlet was consumed during the casting.

History
In the month of Eleasis, of the, Chief Prelate Dayspring of the Clerical Circle of Ravens Bluff declared it illegal for priests to cast summon spectator within city limits due to a raging unnatural storm at the time called the Soulstorm, threatening arrest for those that broke this decree.

## Sulfurus

Sulfurus was a conniving young black dragon who made himself a lair in a cave under an old dried up well in the Western Farmlands outside of Daggerford circa 1372 DR.

Description
Sulfurus was a relatively young black dragon. However, despite his age, Sulfurus was a dangerous opponent, evident from a fine mist of acid droplets that left his mouth with every breath.

Personality
Sulfurus was an evil creature who enjoyed devouring adventurers foolish enough to venture into its lair. His feeling of superiority made him vulnerable to flattery but did not protect his guests from becoming a meal.

Abilities
Sulfurus possessed all abilities of his kin, including true seeing, making it virtually impossible to sneak past him. Magic resistance made him especially dangerous to spellcasters, as well as resistance to normal damage, acid damage, and immunity to paralysis and mind-affecting magic.

Relationships
The black dragon was aware of Gorgomorz's vendetta against Daggerford, and he claimed that the devil was allied with a certain lizardfolk shaman from the Lizard Marsh in order to achieve his goals. Sulfurus, however, considered devils and their endless vendettas silly and amusing.

Possessions
Sulfurus had a small horde accumulated in his lair under the Western Farmlands. It mostly consisted of gemstones and gold.

Apart from compulsive desire to collect gold, Sulfurus collected bones of his victims.

History
Sometime before 1372 DR, Sulfurus found an entrance into the cave that used to be the bottom of an old well that dried up a long time ago. The underwater tunnel stretched from the Lizard Marsh into the cavern. Still, most other creatures would not be capable of traveling via the cave between locations, apart from Sulfurus himself of an extremely capable swimmer. The cavern eventually became the black dragon's lair and housed its hoard. Sulfurus, like many of his kin, had a strong fondness for gold, so creating a lair under a well-known wishing well was a given, with a constant stream of coins being tossed straight into the hoard. During this time underneath the Western Farmlands, the dragon found the old bones of a famous adventurer and a hero, Peredus the Great. Sulfurus desecrated the hero's final resting place, creating an unholy bound, cursing him to be chained to the Prime Material plane as a tortured spectre.

Luckily for Sulfurus, a halfling rogue Burnas Tellrot discovered Peredus's grave and started sending adventurers into the well to recover the fabled Sword of Peredus only to become the black dragon's food. One such adventurer was Dalia, a sailor of the Mariners' Alliance. She survived the dragon's lair by sneaking past the hungry beast and swimming through the tunnel into the Lizard Marsh.

The next unlucky adventurer who agreed to help Burnas Tellrot was the Hero of Daggerford. The adventurer and their companions reached the cruel wyrm but bested him in battle. When defeated, Sulfurus conceded his superiority to the champion and pleaded for his life. Whatever Sulfurus's fate was, the hero managed to find a way out of the lair back to the surface. Some said the black dragon was left alive to grow its bone collection.

## Sulmest's Splendid Shoes & Boots

Sulmest's Splendid Shoes & Boots was a high-quality shoe store in the city of Waterdeep.

Location
This store was located on the High Road, in the North Ward of Waterdeep.

Structure
This structure stood one story tall.

Services
The footwear sold by this store was incredibly expensive, costing as much at ten times the going rate elsewhere in the city. But the footwear in general was always guaranteed to be of good craftsmanship and they charged half the going rate for repairing their products. And many of their boots, which were quite fashionable, could be purchased with a lifelong guarantee of free replacements for any reason, save loss due to theft.

Men's shoes, slippers, and boots for both sexes were custom made to fit their customers' feet, though one could purchase any of their off-the-shelf sizes, which were much cheaper at only twice the average cost for footwear.

Inhabitants
The proprietor, Darion Sulmest, was a high-ranking member of Waterdeep's Order of Cobblers & Corvisers.

## Sujit

Sujit was the imp familiar and a close friend and companion of Delfen Ondabarl circa 1372 DR.

Personality
Sujit was a creature of hellfire; thus, he hated ice and loathed living with his previous master, Mogdor in the icy plains of Etabalon. His experience of being trapped in a chest left the imp with a deep dislike of such containers.

History
Sujit was freed from servitude to his cruel master by Delfen Ondabarl sometime before 1372 DR. The imp befriended the wizard, and they later formed a pact of a wizard and a familiar. Freeing Sujit earned Delfen a bitter enemy in Mogdor, a cruel and strange man with horns and devilish wings who cheerfully declared himself Ondabarl's nemesis. Over the years, Mogdor tried several times to retrieve his stolen property, and in 1372 DR, he came very close to snatching Sujit, magically bounding him and stepping through a gate to Etabalon.

The winged evil wizard hired a bounty hunter from Daggerford, Erik the Fin for the deed. Fin placed a trapped chest in an alley outside the Sorceller's Encapsulate where Delfen Ondabarl often spent his days among his fellow masters of the arcane. The open cast played an alluring ditty that attracted the curious imp, trapping the familiar inside. However, Erik the Fin dropped the chest into Delimbiyr River as he was trying to board a boat back to Mogdor's hideout. Unable to travel searching for the chest, Erik found a mercenary who was willing to - the individual who later would be known as the Hero of Daggerford.

Eventually, the Hero found the said chest. It ended up being washed ashore in one of the coastal caves in the Western Farmlands. Amused by the chest's talkativeness and pleads to be returned to Yellowknife, it was taken back to Sujit's heartbroken friend. After being reunited, Delfen came out with a plan of swift revenge. The wizard placed a trapped and deadly demilich Extallimix inside the chest. The Hero of Daggerford was tasked with returning the chest to Erik the Fin to be passed along to Mogdor. This was believed to be the end of Sujit's old master, who was killed by the angry undead.

## Succor (realm)

Succor was the divine realm of the Pharaonic and Mulhorandi goddess of motherhood and the hearth, Hathor.

Cosmography
Under the Great Wheel cosmology, this divine realm was located within Elysium in the layer of Amoria. It was situated adjacent to the realm of Quietude, a divine realm belonging to Hathor's ally and fellow pantheon member Isis. But in the World Tree cosmology, it was said to be part of Heliopolis and once again close to the realm of Isis, a plane shared by all members of the Mulhorandi pantheon.

Geography
In the Great Wheel's conception of Succor, the realm had a similar terrain to that of Quietude, that being a warm and dry, arid plain. In Heliopolis, the plane was said to be a fertile valley.

Notable Locations
A single, peaceful village stood within the plane.

Inhabitants
The plane was inhabited largely by petitioners that were in life mothers or children, though it regularly had an influx of people seeking refuge.

## Sultlue

The Sultlue  clan were members of the nobility of Waterdeep in the mid-to-late 14 century DR. Their primary legitimate interests were in horse breeding, mercenary work, and trading. Unlike the other noble houses in the City of Splendors, the Sultlues never gave up trading in slaves, managing to keep this and other depraved practices behind a facade of normalcy for centuries. Many of the clan had yuan-ti blood in their veins but did not have any unusual physical features that revealed their heritage.

Organization
The ostensible patriarch of the family, from at least the, to at least the, was Lord Asbrior Sultlue (nicknamed "The Serpent"). His most recent consort was Lady Pera, and his heir was his eldest son, Asbrior II (nicknamed "The Asp"). However, an ancient master yuan-ti pureblood, Sylsinssath Sultlue, resided in the family crypt in the City of the Dead. Sylsinssath was well over a century old in 1372 DR, but he was asleep for great lengths of time. As of this date, the family had seventeen living noble members, not all of whom could be considered completely sane.

There were two factions inside the family—one that maintained a willful ignorance of the shady deeds and depravities of the other. Lord Asbrior, his consort Pera, their daughter Taszeana, and two of their five sons all turned a blind eye toward the scurrilous activities of the rest of the clan, spearheaded by Asbrior's uncles, Darrambur and Pelmaer, and his aunt Jaszmaeril. The entire family considered themselves above the law and were known to hold grudges and be prone to irrational hatred.

Activities
The Sultlues did not merely traffic in slaves, they also experimented on them with poisons, drugs, and scents to develop more potent wares to sell in their trading business. The mistreatment of captured humanoids did not stop there. Slaves that might be recognized were often transformed into beasts of burden and sold for use or for food. For the right price, the Sultlues would polymorph someone into an exact replica of a hated rival or an object of desire for a client to abuse as they wished. Likewise, a duplicate body could be used as convincing evidence to frame someone for various crimes. They even turned slaves into living bombs, placing bags of nails inside their bodies and magically detonating them from a distance. At the top of the long list of perversions, the Sultlues practiced cannibalism, and took particular delight in serving this vile fare to unsuspecting honored guests.

Base of Operations

The Sultlues made their home in a large walled compound of two-story buildings located in the North Ward, fronting on Vondil Street and spanning from Shield Street to Copper Street. To the east, across Copper Street, was the Cragsmere villa, and to the southwest, across Shield Street was the Manthar family compound.

Sylsinssath Sultlue and a number of tainted ones resided in the Sultlue family crypt, located in the City of the Dead. The Sultlue vault was the first major structure south of the Andamaar's Street entrance.

Possessions
Sometime during the 14 century DR, the Sultlues were expecting the shipment from Lushpool of a matched pair of bejeweled daggers of venom via a merchant from Calimshan. Servants of Artor Morlin, the vampire lord of Waterdeep, waylaid the merchant just before the delivery was to be completed and pilfered the fine teak coffer bearing the arms of House Sultlue that held the twin daggers plus a grimoire bound in serpent skin that documented various unwholesome yuan-ti religious rituals. The last known location of these items was in the Lair of the Blood Baron, deep beneath the streets of the city.

Relationships
The Sultlue family were regular customers of Phalantar Orivan, proprietor of Phalantar's Philtres & Components. He provided them with osssra oil, but after the Lords of Waterdeep banned the sale of osssra in the city sometime around 1372 DR, Phalantar tripled the price. House Sultlue was willing to pay up to double the price, but only for future shipments, not stock that had already been ordered. While the merchant and customer haggled over the cost, Phalantar hid his osssra stock in a storeroom in the Dungeon of the Crypt.

Armar Terresk of the City Watch was instrumental in the arrest and conviction of one of Asbrior's sons and a scion of House Thunderstaff sometime around the . Both young men were sentenced to be abandoned in Undermountain and subsequently died, earning Terresk the enmity of the Sultlue clan.

History
The Sultlue family were of Tashlutan descent and venerated Sseth, one of the major gods of the yuan-ti. They were ennobled in Lushpool in the  and at some point the family gained a foothold in Waterdeep, achieving nobility in the . Sylsinssath was from House Eselemas in Lushpool, but had to flee for his life from a rival cousin sometime in the 13 century DR. He made his way to Waterdeep and built another fortune in the City of Splendors. He found kinship in House Sultlue and was venerated by them even as he succumbed to the torpor of old age.

Members
 Asbrior Sultlue: Patriarch in the mid–14 century.
 Pera Sultlue: Consort (third wife) to Asbrior.
 Asbrior Sultlue II: Eldest son of Asbrior.
 Taszeana Sultlue: Young daugher of Asbrior and Pera.
 Darrambur Sultlue: Uncle to Asbrior I.
 Pelmaer Sultlue: Uncle to Asbrior I.
 Jaszmaeril Sultlue: Aunt to Asbrior I.
 Sylsinssath Sultlue: An ancient master yuan-ti pureblood.
 Bromas Sultlue: Family member in the late 15 century.

Family Tree

## Surcoat

Surcoats were a type of loose-fitting outer garment worn, for the purpose of staying warm, by humanoid races overtop of either a cote or tunic.

Description
Surcoats came in a variety of styles. Variations included having sleeves, hoods, capes, or a combination of the three. They were typically made of either linen or samite. Surcoats that were of a white hue were noted as being able to minimize the body's absorption of exterior heat.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold linen surcoats for a price of 6 silver pieces and quilted ones for a price of 2 gold pieces. At a cost of 20 gold pieces they offered a unique variety of hooded surcoat, designed for arctic environments, that they called a "peltcote". Peltcotes reached to below the knees, were made of two layers of quilted wool, lined with fur, lined with pockets and hasps, and came with a belt for tools.

Notable Wearers
Groups
 The livery of the Guild of Trusted Pewterers and Casters included a white sleeveless surcoat.
 During the summer season in Waterdeep, when temperatures reached highs of, the City Guard would dress down from sweltering full body armor into more piece-meal bits of armor worn over white surcoats.
Individuals
 Brant Rethalshield
 Rolf Sunriver
 Sir Corlas
 Torg mac Cei, an Ironlord of Earthfast, wore the same sort of black surcoat as his elite guardsmen.
Nations, Regions, and Settlements
 Daerlun's guardsmen all wore distinct orange surcoats over their plate mail.
 The elite guard of Earthfast's Ironlord all wore black surcoats emblazoned with the symbol of a phoenix and hammer.
 In Ravens Bluff, the inhabitants of the Embassy of Amn all wore surcoats that were sewn with gilded circlets of coarse iron, made to resemble coins.

## Suncatcher Mountain

Suncatcher Mountain was a tall peak in the Galena Mountains.

Geography
The peak was located in the southern reaches of the Galenas near where they met the Earthspur Mountains. It was visible to many in Damara.

History
In the, a strange cloud appeared above Suncatcher Mountain and remained there, not budging even when it was windy. Some in Damara and Vaasa claimed that when the sun was low, they could see a castle atop the cloud. The appearance of the strange cloud prompted Gareth Dragonsbane, then the Baron of Bloodstone, to ask his Spysong organization to gather news about this bizarre occurrence. Riordan Parnell, the leader of Spysong, sent his cousin, Celedon Kierney, to investigate. Celedon was an experienced scout and explorer of the Galenas, but was also somewhat irresponsible. After setting up camp on Suncatcher Mountain, he soon determined that the cloud was home to a kingdom of cloud giants, but rather than report back on his findings, he stayed to explore and perhaps steal some treasure. This prompted Riordan, along with the rest of Spysong, to worry for his safety.

Inhabitants
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, Suncatcher Mountain hosted a cloud-top kingdom of cloud giants who kept cloud dragons as pets.

## Summon Darsson

Summon Darsson was an arcane summoning spell that allowed the caster to communicate with Darsson Spellmaker.

Effects
This spell created an image of Darsson's head before the caster, allowing Darsson to see, speak, and hear as if he was with the caster. Though the spell would wake Darsson if he was sleeping, it would not function if Darsson was unconscious. Darsson provided his close friends with a special phrase word to use when casting the spell, otherwise he might choose not to answer if contacted by a stranger. Darsson sometimes gave strangers a brief moment to explain themselves before he decided to depart.

Darsson could choose to then appear near the caster if he wished, or if he was unable to resist the spell's magic. Either way, he could pick the precise location of his appearance in order to gain some advantage.

Darsson created magical scrolls containing this spell, which he provided to close friends who were not spellcasters.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required Darsson to be conscious to cast.

## Sulasspryn

Sulasspryn was a city in the Moonsea region until it suddenly and violently collapsed into a sinkhole in .

Description
In its heyday, Sulasspryn was a major city and a prosperous seaport, but as of the 14 century DR it was little more than a broken ruin with only a few buildings left standing.

Geography
Sulasspryn lay on the North Coast of the Moonsea, known as a rough and harsh place surrounded by threats from the Galena Mountains, the ogres of Thar, and the untamed land of Vaasa.

Government
Unlike most other cities of the Moonsea, Sulasspryn had a single ruler who was capable of decisive action—one possible reason why the city prospered, for a while, despite the many calamities that befell it.

History
Founding and Growth
Sulasspryn was originally the site of a stronghold belonging to half-elven adventurer Sulass Drowsbane, established some time prior to the 400s DR. Over time, Sulass's stronghold attracted settlers and merchants, and the budding town became part of several caravan routes. In the, Sulasspryn was officially recognized as a city, with Sulass serving as its ruler. In time, his son Galadryl Drowsbane took over the reins. Power remained with Sulass's descendants throughout the centuries, being handed down to the firstborn male heir of each Drowsbane generation.

At its zenith, Sulasspryn was a major city on par with nearby Phlan. As a significant force in the Moonsea region, Sulasspryn was often the subject of depredations from marauding orc clans, roving dragons, pirates, beholders, and even from its neighboring cities. Yet despite the troubled relationship with its neighbors, there was a degree of cooperation between Sulasspryn and the other Moonsea cities. In the, Sulasspryn participated in a grand alliance with Hillsfar, Hulburg, Mulmaster, Phlan, Thentia, Voonlar, Yûlash, and Zhentil Keep to occupy and rebuild the Citadel of the Raven to keep the region safe.

At some point after,This event is not given a precise date in Ruins of Zhentil Keep, but occurred shortly after the founding of the Zhentarim in 1261 DR. Sulasspryn and Hulburg were partially destroyed by dragons, then immediately sacked by orcs. The sage Elminster suspected this was an unintended consequence of a plot by the recently founded Zhentarim to establish themselves as a regional power.

The Destruction of Sulasspryn
Still, the stubborn inhabitants of Sulasspryn rebuilt, just in time for another calamity. In the, during the Moonsea War, Sulasspryn and neighboring Hulburg were both pillaged and razed by forces belonging to Mulmaster. Shortly after, Mulmaster was driven back by an alliance of Hillsfar, Melvaunt, Sembia, Phlan, and Zhentil Keep. Enough citizens and buildings survived the conflict that rebuilding Sulasspryn was considered feasible.

Only a year later, in the, Sulasspryn met its doom. The city suddenly dropped into a large sinkhole, collapsing in on itself and reducing most of its buildings to rubble within less than ten minutes. 7,500 lives were lost that day.

The cause of Sulasspryn's demise was a complex affair engineered by at least two ruinous factions. Firstly, the city's founder, Sulass Drowsbane had made a long list of drow enemies—as his moniker implied. Having witnessed the devastation wreaked on Sulasspryn during the Moonsea War, House Mizzrym sensed weakness and a chance at getting revenge on the descendants of Sulass. The drow allowed the citizens of Sulasspryn to rebuild, thereby wasting their time and effort. Meanwhile, House Mizzrym used their slaves to tunnel under the city. When enough progress had been made, they invoked the power of their dark goddess, Lolth. Sulasspryn was destroyed immediately.

At the same time as the drow dug their tunnels, a similar plot was enacted by the eye tyrant Alarkanamace. As a participant in the Vellurith, an ancient game of manipulation played among beholderkind, Alarkanamace sought to accomplish one of its goals: to destroy a human city so that it is never rebuilt. The rules of the Vellurith meant that the eye tyrant could not destroy the city directly, so Alarkanamace had to trick others into doing the deed. It subtly manipulated duergar miners into digging beneath Sulasspryn, compelling them to lure purple worms ever closer to the surface to bore their tunnels. It also tricked the dwarven watermaster Burdurin Longhammer into believing he had found ancient schematics detailing a subterranean river diversion—in reality just an area carved out and magically aged by Alarkanamace to further weaken the structural integrity of the area beneath Sulasspryn. Finally, the beholder saturated the limestone rock separating the duergar mines from the city above, then spread rumors of rich gem deposits beneath Sulasspryn to encourage its citizens to start digging. All of these events served to destabilize the very ground on which the city stood, and before long the city collapsed into the resulting massive sinkhole.

Whether the cause of Sulasspryn's collapse was a drow revenge plot or the subtle machinations of a beholder—or a combination of the two—the city was utterly ruined and would never rise again.It is unknown if these two plots interfaced with each other in any way. Still, the fact that Alarkanamace's scheme was rewarded with a boon from the Great Mother, the deity of beholders, indicates that its contribution to the collapse of Sulasspryn was substantial, and possibly the primary cause. Several members of the Drowsbane family survived, but none ever saw fit to reclaim their legacy.

After the Fall
The ruins of Sulasspryn were overtaken by forces belonging to House Mizzrym, led by Azanza Mizzrym, who used it as a staging point for nightly raids to the surface to kidnap slaves. Although the city seemed desolate and abandoned during the day, it came alive at night: Sulasspryn was crawling with drow patrols, their summoned Abyssal servants, various creatures from the Underdark, and enormous quantities of spiders. As of the late 15 century DR, Sulasspryn was still notorious for being haunted by spiders.

In the, Sulasspryn was the site of negotiations between the Cult of the Dragon and the Zhentarim; both factions hoped to form an alliance and together conquer the land of Thar.

Rumors & Legends
The ruins of Sulasspryn were said to hold great treasure buried beneath the surface, which meant that each year, at least one band of adventurers was drawn there. The fact that some of these adventurers never returned only encouraged their peers, as their disappearance indicated they had found something—or something found them.

Along with the nearby ruins of Hulburg, shattered Sulasspryn served as a stern reminder to the people of the Moonsea that humankind was not entirely secure in the region.

Notable Locations

 The Drowsbane castle was destroyed when the city fell, save for a single -tall tower. The upper third floor of this tower was filled with phosphorescent fungi by the drow that glowed so intensely that it appeared to be a lighthouse to ships passing nearby on the Moonsea. Unwary ships were steered aground so the drow could rob and enslave the unfortunate sailors.
 A white marble temple to Selûne lay in ruins after Sulasspryn's collapse. Drow forces defiled what remained of its interior and erected a shrine to Lolth within. During three days of the full moon, the ruined temple was bathed in intense moonlight forcing the drow and their spiders away from the area.
 The city's Old Well was connected to the tunnels dug by the drow, leading to House Mizzrym's primary outpost in the area and eventually all the way to Menzoberranzan.
 The many spiders of fallen Sulasspryn were concentrated in the city's gigantic granary, which had survived the destruction mostly unscathed.

Inhabitants
Once a bustling city, the ruins of Sulasspryn came to be mostly inhabited by drow forces loyal to House Mizzrym, their various servants from the Underdark and the Abyss, and large quantities of spiders.

## Sunlit Wetlands

The Sunlit Wetlands, also known as the Putrid Bog, was a stretch of wilderness situated along the northern bank of the River Chionthar in the Western Heartlands of Faerûn. While it first appeared as a wild but beauteous bit of nature, it was truly a place of dread and misery, home to malevolent dark fey creatures and evil magic.

Description
At first glace, the wetlands appeared as a serene and perhaps overgrown fen, ideal as a place to rest. This was merely an illusion however, as they were in fact a foul and deadly swamp full of peril.

Geography
The Sunlit Wetlands were situated along the river south of the Risen Road, just west of the village of Moonhaven.

Geographical Features

The swamp was difficult to navigate, with few muddy pathways winding through murky waters dotted and twisting vines. Dead and rotting trees choked off passageways as overgrown root systems sprang up over the mud and rock.

A rudimentary set of docks and wooden walkways were built around the southern end of the wetlands, with a bridge that extended out to a small islet in the southern waters. A single massive willow tree grew within the islet, the base of which was encircled with stone slabs inscribed swirling, almost pictographic designs. Several crumbling stone menhirs encircled the willow, their origin unknown.

Flora & Fauna
A small flock of sheep grazed across the wetlands, innocently "baah-ing" at anyone that stopped close to them. These seemingly adorable sheep were in fact vicious redcaps with powerful illusion magic placed over them.

Large barbed bulrush plants dotted the wetlands, with tantalizing apples that were ripe for the taking. The apples left an upset feeling in the stomach of anyone daring enough to bite into them.

These plants left gaping wounds to anyone plucking the fruit not dexterous enough to avoid their barbs. If not treated properly in short order, these wounds could easily turn to rot.

History
As of the, the islet with the standing stones served as a secret dead drop site for the Shadow Druids. It was used by the druid Kagha to communicate with others of her order during her infiltration of the Emerald Grove enclave to the northeast.

Notable Locations
 Riverside Teahouse, the quaint and charming abode of the manipulative and hate-filled green hag Auntie Ethel.

Inhabitants
Several wood woads and a pair of ancient mud mephits made their home in the area around the great willow tree.

## Sunrise Mountains (North Faerûn)

The Sunrise Mountains, also known as the Taurax Mountains,This alternative name seems to appear only in The Great Glacier (1992), so it could be reasonably inferred that this is the Sunrise Mounatins' name in the Ulutiun language. were a mountain range in the far Moonsea North.

Geography
The Sunrise Mountains lay north of the Frozen Forest and the Great Mount of Ghaethluntar, situated between the High Ice of the Anauroch to the west and the Great Glacier to the east. It was the furthest north mountain range in the region.

History
In the, the Sunrise Mountains served as the northern border of the Tyrantfog that spread out from Zhentil Keep.

## Sun Festival

The Sun Festival is one of the two most important celebrations for the inhabitants of the Shining Plains in the Vilhon Reach.

Description
Along with the Festival of the Hunt, the Sun Festival was celebrated annually. The Sun Festival's goal was to honor plainsmen who resided outside the cities or travelers from other lands and appreciate their contributions to the society of the Shining Plains. Both festivals were extremely important to the plainsmen as they always tried to travel back home for both celebrations

The plainsmen strongly believed in using the Sun Festival to celebrate outsiders who performed actions of great importance to the Shining Plains. Such deeds included an adventurer taking down a dangerous beast by themselves, like a saber-toothed tiger, or a troupe of outsiders killing a monstrous creature such as a manticore.

The festival took place on the last day of Eleasias, referred to as the Highsun in the Shining Plains. These celebrations started with honored guests and heroes being accepted into the community as honorary citizens and continued into the night, filled with dance and food. Every outsider hero cut their hand and dripping blood onto the ground. When the blood was spilled honoring the land, the cut was swaddled, usually by a local female. During the rest of the night, heroes shook hands with the plainsmen and partook in revelries.

References

## Sunglade

The Sunglade was a clearing wherein ancient standing stones still stood in the Yuirwood. The circle of standing stones in the Sunglade was the oldest circle in the Yuirwood.

Description
This was a sunny, pleasant glade well within the Yuirwood, guarded by ancient willows and inhabited only by songbirds and butterflies. Within the center of the Sunglade was a large circle of stone menhirs with a second, smaller circle of ten menhirs in its center. Both sets of menhirs were covered in elaborate zoomorphic images and ancient Espruar runes. Both sets were magically warded against age, so they seemed almost new. Each menhir of the outer circle was dedicated to a different member of the Seldarine, while each of the inner ten was dedicated to a deity of the Yuir pantheon.

Geography
The Sunglade was located in the interior uplands of the Yuirwood.

History
The menhirs of the Sunglade were erected by the Yuir elves before they abandoned the place, but their enemies—trolls and drow—could not destroy the place, as those who tried died or disappeared.

As of 1368 DR, the explorer Talyssa Strongbow still felt a presence there of some sort, reportedly both distant, ancient, and hostile; and around the same time some half-elven tribes of the Yuirwood had begun returning to the circle to worship the ancient gods honored there.

## Summon nature's ally I

Summon nature's ally I was the most basic of the series of summon nature's ally spells available to druid and ranger spellcasters.

Effects
This spell functioned just like all summon nature's ally spells, which in turn resembled the summon monster spells. Creatures could only be summoned into an environment in which they could survive (i.e., in which it could move and breathe). Summoned creatures would defend their summoner to the best of their ability, and if the caster could communicate with the summoned creature, then they could give it more complex commands.

As the weakest of the summon nature's ally spells, it could be used to summon a single dire rat, eagle, monkey, octopus, owl, porpoise, small viper, or wolf.

Components
The spell required a divine focus and both verbal and somatic components.

## Sunstone (magic item)

Sunstones were elven magic items originating on the island of Evermeet in both the Eagle and Silver Hills.

Description
These amber-colored stones emitted light. They were easily cut and shaped like gemstones and used as light sources on Evermeet.

Powers
Sunstones emitted light for many years. The amount of light emitted depending on the size of the stone, with smaller stones producing an amount of light similar to a candle while larger ones could easily illuminate an entire room.

Appendix
References

## Sunblight

Sunblight was an ambitious duergar clan residing in and beneath the Spine of the World mountains of Faerûn.

Base of Operations
To support their expansion into the surface of Faerûn, the clan erected the fortress Sunblight in the Spine of the World, serving as a kind of gateway between the clan's Underdark holdings and the surface.

History
Under the leadership of Xardorok Sunblight, the Sunblight clan expanded aggressively into the surface world. Xardorok's ambition was stoked by Asmodeus, who adopted the guise of the duergar deity Deep Duerra to manipulate the dwarven warlord into conquering Icewind Dale.

Relationships
The Sunblight clan was fraught with murder and intrigue. Xardorok Sunblight was married three times and had nine children in total. He killed his first wife, Thizrun, and first son, Ulthoon, for plotting against him. Yrraska, his second wife, was killed in a tunnel collapse, while his third wife Marral died fighting mind flayers. Nearly all of his remaining children died in these two incidents as well, leaving him with only two living sons as of the .

Xardorok's aspirations for the Sunblight clan were ultimately foiled, and their fortress was taken over by a tribe of kobolds.

Notable Members
 Xardorok Sunblight, an ambitious warlord.
 Durth Sunblight, Xardorok's son and lieutenant.
 Nildar Sunblight, Xardorok's youngest son and lieutenant.

Family Tree

## Sunwatcher

Sunwatchers were priests of the Azuposi people of the Pasocada Basin who tracked the sun through the seasons.

Activities
These priests performed a variety of rituals and ceremonies throughout the year to keep the Azuposi people within the sun's graces. Their duty kept them busy at all times, preventing them from traveling or adventuring. Among their most important rituals was sun watching.

Culture
A sunwatcher connected his people to the mighty sun, which was considered to be the most powerful spirit by the Azuposi.

Abilities
Sunwatchers were skilled in the use of talismanic magic. They could not turn undead.

Possessions
Sunwatchers wore a white and gold poncho that signified their status. They wielded spears, short bows, and daggers, all of which were sanctified during a special ceremony before use. The spear and short bow represented the piercing rays of the sun to a sunwatcher.

Appendix
References

## Sunmasters (Thay)

The Sunmasters were an elite contingent within the Thayan military active during the late 15 century DR. The group was mostly made up of Banite clerics and warlocks specializing in radiant magics.

Activities
The Sunmasters were sworn to serve Thay, and were led by Corporal Isma Lecere. They were trained as an elite strike force specializing in dealing with Thay's Shadovar enemies during the Shadovar–Thay War. As such, they rarely became involved in anything but the most important or the most desperate battles.

Base of Operations
The Sunmasters were assigned to Surcross, a well-defended town on Thay's western border. They had their own special chambers within the town's fortified garrison, which were unusually opulent and cushy. They had comfortable beds, thick carpets, luxurious clothes, and fine food and wine, even while much of the town starved during martial law.

History
In the, Surcross came under attack by the flying Shadovar citadel of Kolthunral, resulting in a long and grueling battle. Even so, the Sunmasters remained largely aloof, only engaging in combat when circumstances called for their elite talents.

Appendix
References

---

## Surcross

Surcross was a heavily fortified town in western Thay on the border with Thesk.

Geography
The town was located on the border of Thesk and Thay. It lay at the base of the Thayan Plateau east of the Thesk Mountains and south of the River Sur and the Sur Hills. Prior to the Spellplague, the town lay along the road from Nethentir to Two Stars. Following the Spellplague, this region became craggy and broken, and the skies were often choked with smoke from the Thaymount. As such, the area came to be known as the Bonedark Hills.

Government
Surcross was controlled by a Thayan military governor who ruled from the town's central citadel, the Tombstone. In the late 15 century DR, this was Ukulsid, who would entertain his people's pleas and pass judgment on their disputes within the Tombstone's Hall of Petition. However, he was known to conscript or kill any petitioners who so much as annoyed him with their needs. Ukulsid was loyal to Szass Tam.

The Thayan army was in complete control of the government and the town watch. Those who violated the law could expect to be imprisoned or expelled if lucky, but more likely executed and reanimated to serve in the army.

Trade
In the late 15 century DR, Surcross served as the eastern anchor of the Thayan's so-called "Shadowfell Road", which connected it to the Dread Ring in Neverwinter Wood and served as a key supply line for Valindra Shadowmantle's nefarious operations in the Sword Coast North.

Defenses
Surcross was heavily fortified as of the late 15 century DR, and boasted a larger garrison than most cities many times its size. This was for the simple reason that Szass Tam, in his paranoia, saw the town as the first line of defense in the case of an unlikely assault on Thay from the west. The town and surrounding area were well-patrolled by Thayan forces both living and undead. Some of these forces rode chariots pulled either by undead creatures or propelled wholly by magic. The most elite troops were the Sunmasters, evil clerics and warlocks who wielded radiant energies.

The town was ringed by a heavy stone wall nearly  thick and dozens of feet high. This was known as the Wall of Crypts, so named because of the hundreds of corpses stored within the wall to serve as an emergency reserve army of zombies should the wall ever be breached. The main gate, known as the Gates of Bone, was made from the bones of humanoids and monsters, and could animate as a fearsome undead construct to defend the town.

Five thin, windowless towers—hundreds of feet tall but only a few dozen feet in diameter—stood equidistant from the town's center along the perimeter. These were known as the Fingers of Szass Tam, and employed a number of powerful defensive magics. The walls were enchanted to allow defenders to see and hurl spells from inside, but were solid marble to any attackers from outside. In addition, the Fingers had the ability to project fields of force, which could serve either as bridges to quickly deploy troops around the city or as shields to protect from areal assaults (although such a shield could only spread across the whole city for a few minutes at a time).

History
Because of its location, many renegade Red Wizards were known to pass through Surcross while fleeing west out of Thay.

The entire region surrounding the town was devastated during the Spellplague in the late 14 century DR, and it was not until the late 15 century DR that the Thayans began rebuilding roads to a number of the ruined towns in the area, including Surcross.

By the, Surcross was the largest Thayan settlement west of the Thayan Plateau, and had become a fortified base for the Thayan army. It was one of several sites where fortresses similar to Dread Rings were being constructed at the behest of Szass Tam in apparent preparation for war with Thesk. At this time, town's garrison was comprised of soldiers loyal to Valindra Shadowmantle and Ukulsid—both of whom in turn served Szass Tam—however servants of Dempharis Sibront, the self-proclaimed "zulkir in exile", had begun to infiltrate their ranks.

That same year, as part of the ongoing Shadovar–Thay War, Surcross came under attack from the floating Netherese citadel of Kolthunral. The city's forces proved largely insufficient to repel this assault, and soon the town's vast garrison had been reduced to half strength, even accounting for the practice of reanimating slain soldiers or civilians to add to the undead ranks. The lands around the town were reduced to a necromantic wasteland of blood and body parts known as the Charnel Fields, and only the protective shields of the Fingers of Szass Tam prevented the town from being completely obliterated by areal bombardment. As the situation became more dire, the Thayan army instituted martial law, locking down the town, requisitioning nearly all remaining food, and brutally repressing any dissent or disrespect in the populace, no matter how mild. During this siege, the town was said to be choked in hot smoke and eerily quiet.

Notable Locations
 The Fingers of Szass Tam, five highly magical guard towers around the town's perimeter.
 The Garrison, a heavily fortified series of barracks for the Thayan troops, which contained teleportation circles to allow for speedy deployment around the city.
Gates of Bone, the main passage through the town's defensive wall.
The Tombstone, the central citadel from which the town was ruled. The top floor contained an opulent throne room wherein the town's leaders could commune with Szass Tam, while the cellar contained a massive ossuary used for necromantic experiments.

Inhabitants
As of the late 15 century DR, the citizens of the town were outnumbered by the Thayan soldiers stationed there. It was home to humans as well as dwarves, gnolls, half-orcs, and orcs.

Notable Inhabitants
 Isma Lecere, a corporal in the Thayan military who led the elite Sunmasters contingent in the late 1470s DR.
 Omdros, a half-orc death mage who led the saboteurs loyal to Dempharis Sibront embedded within the Thayan ranks in the late 1470s DR.
 Ukulsid, an undead gnoll who served as the town's military governor in the late 1470s DR.

## Sun watching

Sun watching was a religious skill used by the Azuposi people of the Pasocada Basin of Maztica.

Description
Only the Azuposi sunwatchers, special priests dedicated to appeasing the mighty sun spirit Sun Father, practiced sun watching. Sunwatchers used this skill to understand specific dates with regard to the sun's position compared to known landmarks. Knowing the correct date ensured the sunwatcher could perform the appropriate ritual or ceremony for that precise time of the year.

The basic concepts behind sun watching could also be used with the moon and stars.

## Sus'sistinako

Sus'sistinako, who was also referred to as the Spider Woman, was a great spirit of the folklore and myth of the Azuposi people of the Pasocada Basin of northern Maztica.

History
Sus'sistinako, along with the Sun, was one of the original spirits. It was believed by the Azuposi that each of these beings was somehow the parent of the other. The pair created the land that would become the Pasocada Basin out of the primeval ocean.

Following the creation of the land, Sus'sistinako used her song to create other spirits of creation including Iyatiku, the Corn Mother, and Alosaka, the Germinator.

## Sultan Street

Sultan Street was a busy market road in Khaerbaal, a port city of Halruaa.

Description
Sultan Street stood within walking distance from the docks of Khaerbaal. Like many other places in the city, massive bilboa trees loomed over the street's many shops, stalls, and workshops. Long colorful silk banners were used as shop signs that made the street a vibrant sight to behold. Narrow alleyways between closely nestled buildings were almost invisible to casual observers. Many of the alleyway entrances and buildings were covered in thick, fragrant flowering vines. Some were thick and sturdy enough to be climbed.

Several blocks down the street stood a large island of thick greenery—a public garden built around another sprawling bilboa tree.

## Summon monster IV

Summon monster IV, also known as monster summoning IV or summon creature IV, was one of the series of summon monster spells available to spellcasters.

Effects
This spell functioned similar to the other summon monster spells. It spell could conjure dire spiders, and creatures from other planes of existence, such as lantern archons, mephits, yeth hounds, howlers, or any type of grue. It could also bring about celestial giant owls, giant eagles, or lions; it could also conjure fiendish dire wolves, giant wasps, giant praying mantises, large sharks, monstrous spiders the size of horses, or vipers the size of elephants. Sharks could only be summoned into water; the creatures could be substituted by those summoned by lesser spells in the series, but increasing their numbers in return.

Prior to the, could summon between one and three monsters within a  radius of the caster. Those monsters could be ankhegs, carnivorous apes, Banderlogs, Ettercaps, Gargoyles, Ghasts, Hell hounds, Huecuvas, hydras with 5 heads, werewolves, Ogres, gray oozes, owlbears, giant scorpions the size of a hut, giant constrictor snakes, poisonous toads, fire toads, giant wasps, worgs and yetis. If cast into salt water, its summons could be answered by hippocampi, kapoacinths, giant crayfish, merrows, giant otters, sharks, tritons, or yellow urchins. In fresh water, it could instead bring giant water beetles, giant crayfish, kapoacinths, kelpies, merrows, giant otters, giant pikes, or giant water spiders.

Components
This spell had the material component of a small bag and candle.

## Succor of Berronar

Succor of Berronar was a spell used by clerics of Berronar Truesilver.

Effects
When cast, this powerful spell duplicated the effects of several other spells: cure blindness or deafness, cure disease, cure serious wounds, neutralize poison and repair injury. In addition, the spell postponed lycanthropy and addictions for a whole day. The spell also restored sanity and gave the recipient a sense of general well being. This spell could only be cast once per month.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of Berronar Truesilver and a single drop of holy water.

## Summon monster VII

Summon monster VII, also known as monster summoning VII, was a conjuration spell that called a creature to fight for the caster. The version that was more popular by the Year of Wild Magic summoned outsiders; prior, the most popular version summoned natural monsters instead. It was also the most powerful version of the summon monster series known up to that year.

Effects
Summon monster VII functioned similarly to the spell able to be cast by less experienced casters: summon monster I. However, this more powerful spell allowed the caster to summon monsters such as avorals, djinni, invisible stalkers, red slaadi, bone devil or babau. It could also summon elementals the size of elephants, celestial elephants, or fiendish monstrous scorpions the size of elephants, megaraptors and girallons. In the water, it could summon celestial baleen whales or fiendish giant octopi. It yielded one of those creatures; alternatively, it could be used to summon a greater number of monsters from a lower level spell.

The version most popular prior to  summoned instead a number of monsters native to the Prime instead. The number varied between one and three in all cases, and there was a delay of a minute: the summons could be answered by basilisks, behirs or tunnel worms, bulettes, chimeras, ettins or hill giants, flesh golems, gorgons, hydras with ten heads, pyrohydras or cryohydras of eight heads, fire lizards, mummies, neo-otyughs, black puddings, ropers, shambling mounds, giant slugs, hieracosphinxes, 2-headed trolls, umber hulks or xorns. Up to two would appear.

The spell's yield in saltwater was instead manta rays, giant sea slugs, giant sea snakes, or giant squids. In freshwater, the spell summoned giant catfishes, green hags, water nagas, or giant sea snakes.

The earlier spell could also be made to summon even stronger monsters: by increasing the delay to two minutes and allowing for only a single monster to be summoned, the creature would instead number among rhinoceros beetles, fire giants, frost giants, fomorians, stone golems, gorgimeras, 12-headed hydras or 8-headed lernaean hydras, brown puddings, remorhazes, rust monsters, will-o'-wisps, or even purple worms.

The increased delay, in fresh water, resulted in the summons answered by giant sea slugs, dragon turtles, kuo-toa monitors, and mottled worms. In salt water, the effect was even more drastic, and could bring forth eyes of the deep, dragon turtles, krakens, giant sharks, the largest of vodyanoi, killer whales, small herbivore whales, and mottled worms. However, the caster ran a certain risk when they summoned a kraken or a vodyanoi. Those creatures were too willful for the spell's power to control them completely, and if commanded to act against their nature, there was a slim chance they might spurn the spell's yoke on their intentions and act free-willed for as long as the summon lasted.

Descriptor note
When this was used to summon creatures of a certain type, the spell itself would be considered a spell of the creature's type.

History
The spell was attributed to Netherese arcanist Lucke in  and was originally called Lucke's 7th monster summons.

## Surd

Surd was a small agricultural town in the eastern edges of Sembia several miles away inland from the Dragon Reach.

Government
The town of Surd followed the greater laws of Sembia. The town held strict laws regarding banditry. All highwaymen caught in the act, especially those who preyed on merchants and caravans, were swiftly convicted and executed by hanging.

Trade
Trade traffic moved between Selgaunt and towns of Ordulin, Surd, and Tulbegh along Rauthauvyr's Road and entered the capital through its North Gate. Ordulin was connected with Surd through Taela's Trail, while the Coastwatch connected Surd with the towns of Yhaunn and Tulbegh. Because of its position, Surd was a busy merchant caravan stop.

Along with Tulbegh, Surd supported fishing communities that sprang along the Dragon Reach on the rich and flat shores that made the region's development an easy task. However, that flat land made the lands around Surd and its neighbors quite unscenic.

History
Sometime before the, Tyranthraxus, the Possessing Spirit, sent his spies and assassins to Surd in search of powerful ioun stones. His agents returned empty-handed after scouring through Surd and entire Sembia.

Circa the, then 20-year-old Artus Cimber braved the tunnels underneath Surd, infiltrating the town's prison to rescue Scoril Cimber, his renowned highwayman father. Not their first jailbreak, Artus freed his father before he was executed for his crimes against Sembia.

Circa the, a suspicious merchant arrived in Surd after making stops in Yhaunn and Tulbegh. The man came selling exquisite pieces of authentic sahuagin jewelry and art, including a dwarf-sized stature. The man claimed to be on a mission to share the "beauty of the sea" with the poorer coastal communities. He was selling his art for a stunningly low price. However, soon after he left Surd, its inhabitants discovered that an angry sahuagin tribe was searching for their stolen valuables. The identity of the shady merchant remained unknown, but the coastal cities of Surd and Tulbegh offered a reward for capturing the man whose appearance was likely a magical illusion created by an enchanted ring. Some believed that this stolen jewelry sale was an intentionally malicious act aimed at stressing the relationship between human and sahuagin communities of the Dragon Reach.

Sometime in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, a reclusive wizard Rallowglass Hynraun, inventor of several enchanted staves, was murdered, and his tower was ransacked and broken out of. Some said a mysterious being summoned by the wizard or something magically animated by him slew Rallowglass and got away with vandalizing his home and stealing his research. One particularly outrageous rumor claimed it was a tarrasque. However, no sage agreed that such a beast would ever be interested in stealing rings, scrolls, wands, potions, and other magical miscellanies. One thing was known for sure, and the killer stone had enough magic that it could easily hide in plain sight and strike again. Several well-known wizards of Sembia inspected the ransacked tower. Still, locals claimed that these wizards were looking for a hint on a hidden stash or a secret refuge of Rallowglass Hynraun's, somewhere on Faerûn, or even in the planes. The kill was sure to attack again if that secret refuge was ever to be uncovered.

During the Sembian civil war that followed the return of Thultanthar of the  nobles of Surd, Yhaunn, Tulbeg, and Ornstar, attended the moot in the city of Ordulin in regarding the looming war.

## Sun arrow

Sun arrow was a type of offensive arcane spell from the southern nation of wizards – Halruaa.

Effects
When summoned, the spell's energies formed a heated arrow of sunlight that spun towards its target. The sun arrow left charred and smoking burn wounds on its target but was not necessarily lethal but still agonizingly painful. Like with most other types of magic, Halruaan jordainis were trained to defend themselves from the sun arrow spell with a single smooth motion. However, there was a mundane method of countering the spell. A reflective surface, like a shard of glass, a mirror, or a polished brass platter, reflected the spell and, with a skillful application, could be sent back onto its caster.

History
In the, a young talented spell-slinger Tzigone, taught her jordaini ally Matteo to use reflective surfaces to harmlessly bounce sun arrow spells away. The skill he used to his master's, Procopio Septus' delight, to deflect magics back at the "assassin" sent for testing the young jordaini's skills. A simple use of a polished dish left the wizard rolling on the ground in agony and in need of a cleric.

## Sunfish

Sunfish was the name given to several species of fresh and saltwater fish.

Description
Species of sunfish had a distinct flat and round shape, making them a popular decorative motif in the ancient Netheril. Sunfish traveled and fed in schools, sometimes found in the shallows, hunting minnows who came up from under the banks towards sunlight. Larger species of sunfish were known for their pungent urine.

Habitats
Freshwater ponds of the forest of Cormanthor was home to a series of sunfish that was considered a game fish by locals. A species of tadpole-sized sunfish inhabited Arabellan ponds and marshes.

Usages
Sunfish stew was a fanciful dish prepared for the patriars of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

## Sure striking short sword

Sure striking short swords were short swords enhanced with the sure striking enchantment.

Description
Sure striking short swords varied in design and all appeared to be quite simple double-edged pointed blades, not much bigger than a dagger –  long.

Powers
The enchantments placed on the swords made them dramatically easier to wield and land a strike on a target. Even though their damage potential was in line with a simple +1 enchantment, their magically enchanted aim was comparable to a powerful +5 enchantment.

History
In the, one such sword was carried by Artemus of Shaengarne Ford in Icewind Dale. His home was ransacked and broken into building materials by the orc invaders of the Broken Tusk Clan that took his village hostage. However, Arte's stock remained unmolested as orcs failed to find the storage cellar of his shop where the sure striking short sword could be found, along with several less valuable items and enchanted +1 throwing hammer and a magical +1 flail.

This never-wilting wreath was made out of woven together laurel leaves and roses.

## Sunset House

Sunset House was a strange house located in the city of Zhentil Keep during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
The house was located in south Zhentil Keep in the eastern section of the Foreign Quarter right next to the South Cemetery.

Description
Stone gargoyles sat atop the roof of Sunset House. All of the windows were covered with heavy black drapes for privacy.

Interior
A secret passage in the cellar connected to ancient ruins located beneath Zhentil Keep, which in turn connected to tunnels in the Underdark.

Defenses
A glyph of warding sealed all of the doors and windows of Sunset House. It would trigger when any non-drow attempted to enter and turn them to stone.

Inhabitants
A group of drow from House Mizzrym dwelled within Sunset House following the destruction of Zhentil Keep in the . Only Lord Orgauth and the clergy of Loviatar knew of their presence in the city. Lord Orgauth, who, unbeknownst to the drow, was actually a pit fiend in disguise at this time, sold undesirables as slaves to the dark elves. Kebella Mizzrym led the drow stationed here. A sorcerer named Taybar Rozziss and a mind flayer named Xog aided her.
